This is used by mode monitors to record the\n    modal power or amplitude as a function of time or frequency.\n\n    The overlap is:\n        overlap = Re[integral(E_tangential × H_mode* · n̂) dA]\n\n    where E_tangential is the field at the monitor plane, H_mode* is the\n    conjugate of the mode's magnetic field, and n̂ is the normal direction.\n\n    Args:\n        field_components: Dict of field arrays at monitor placements\n        mode_solution: ModeSolution with Ex, Ey, Ez, Hx, Hy, Hz arrays\n        normal_axis: Normal axis for the cross-section\n        placements: Monitor cell indices\n\n    Returns:\n        Complex overlap amplitude\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n\n    # Get mode coordinates\n    mode_x = mode_solution.x\n    mode_y = mode_solution.y\n    mode_nx = len(mode_x)\n    mode_ny = len(mode_y)\n\n    def to_complex(c: tuple) -> complex:\n        return complex(c[0], c[1]) if isinstance(c, tuple) else complex(c, 0.0)\n\n    def get_mode_field(field_name: str, i: int, j: int) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"Get mode field at grid point (i, j).\"\"\"\n        if j * mode_nx + i >= len(getattr(mode_solution, field_name)):\n            return 0.0 + 0.0j\n        return to_complex(getattr(mode_solution, field_name)[j * mode_nx + i])\n\n    overlap = 0.0 + 0.0j\n\n    for i, idx in enumerate(placements):\n        # Compute mode grid indices (nearest neighbor interpolation)\n        # This is a simplified mapping - real implementation would use proper interpolation\n        mx_idx = min(i % mode_nx, mode_nx - 1)\n        my_idx = min(i // mode_nx, mode_ny - 1)\n\n        # Get field components at this point\n        if normal_axis == 0:\n            # x-normal: tangential fields are Ey, Ez, Hy, Hz\n            ex_field = 0.0  # No Ex for x-normal\n            ey_field = field_components.get(\"Ey\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            ez_field = field_components.get(\"Ez\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hx_field = 0.0  # No Hx for x-normal\n            hy_field = field_components.get(\"Hy\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hz_field = field_components.get(\"Hz\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n\n            mEy = get_mode_field(\"Ey\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mEz = get_mode_field(\"Ez\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHy = get_mode_field(\"Hy\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHz = get_mode_field(\"Hz\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n\n            # Poynting vector S = E × H*\n            # Normal component (x): S_x = E_y*H_z* - E_z*H_y\n            s_normal = ey_field * np.conj(hz_field) - ez_field * np.conj(hy_field)\n            m_s_normal = mEy * np.conj(mHz) - mEz * np.conj(mHy)\n\n        elif normal_axis == 1:\n            # y-normal: tangential fields are Ex, Ez, Hx, Hz\n            ex_field = field_components.get(\"Ex\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            ey_field = 0.0  # No Ey for y-normal\n            ez_field = field_components.get(\"Ez\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hx_field = field_components.get(\"Hx\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hy_field = 0.0  # No Hy for y-normal\n            hz_field = field_components.get(\"Hz\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n\n            mEx = get_mode_field(\"Ex\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mEz = get_mode_field(\"Ez\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHx = get_mode_field(\"Hx\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHz = get_mode_field(\"Hz\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n\n            # Normal component (y): S_y = E_z*H_x* - E_x*H_z*\n            s_normal = ez_field * np.conj(hx_field) - ex_field * np.conj(hz_field)\n            m_s_normal = mEz * np.conj(mHx) - mEx * np.conj(mHz)\n\n        else:  # axis == 2\n            # z-normal: tangential fields are Ex, Ey, Hx, Hy\n            ex_field = field_components.get(\"Ex\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            ey_field = field_components.get(\"Ey\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            ez_field = 0.0  # No Ez for z-normal\n            hx_field = field_components.get(\"Hx\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hy_field = field_components.get(\"Hy\", np.zeros(n_pts))[i]\n            hz_field = 0.0  # No Hz for z-normal\n\n            mEx = get_mode_field(\"Ex\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mEy = get_mode_field(\"Ey\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHx = get_mode_field(\"Hx\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n            mHy = get_mode_field(\"Hy\", mx_idx, my_idx)\n\n            # Normal component (z): S_z = E_x*H_y* - E_y*H_x*\n            s_normal = ex_field * np.conj(hy_field) - ey_field * np.conj(hx_field)\n            m_s_normal = mEx * np.conj(mHy) - mEy * np.conj(mHx)\n\n        # Add contribution to overlap integral\n        overlap += s_normal * np.conj(m_s_normal)\n\n    return overlap\n\n\ndef record_mode_time_domain(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    monitor_state,\n    mode_solutions: tuple,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Record mode overlap amplitudes at the current timestep.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        monitor_state: ModeMonitorState with compiled placement and mode info\n        mode_solutions: Tuple of ModeSolution objects for overlap\n        time: Current simulation time\n    \"\"\"\n    if not hasattr(monitor_state, \"compiled\"):\n        return\n\n    compiled = monitor_state.compiled\n\n    if not compiled.is_time_domain:\n        return\n\n    # Extract field components at monitor placements\n    field_components = extract_mode_field_components(\n        electric_field, magnetic_field,\n        compiled.placements, compiled.normal_axis\n    )\n\n    # Compute overlap for each mode\n    monitor_state.record_mode_time_domain(field_components, mode_solutions, time)\n\n\ndef record_mode_frequency_domain(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,","numLines":200,"startLine":400,"totalLines":849}}}

Prefer staged update kernels and capability-driven specialization by region or chunk.\n35\t- Every substantive implementation task should include or update tests, examples, benchmarks, or instrumentation that exercise the new behavior.\n36\t- When a Tidy3D feature is not yet supported, implement or document explicit validation or error behavior rather than leaving silent gaps.\n37\t- Record initialization time, JIT time, warm steady-state timestep metrics, and cells-updated-per-second style metrics whenever solver execution paths become runnable.\n38\t- Use local reference repos and papers aggressively for research, but do not copy protected code or documentation into this repository.\n39\t- Assume the current machine has at least two NVIDIA GPUs available and use that for meaningful smoke tests where feasible.\n40\t\n41\t## Output Contract\n42\t\n43\t- `summary` should be a concise outcome statement.\n44\t- `key_findings` should capture the most important concrete discoveries or decisions.\n45\t- `artifacts` should list meaningful outputs with both `path` and `description`.\n46\t- `error_summary` should be filled for `blocked` or `failed` runs and empty otherwise.\n47\t- `next_action` should be `retry`, `human_review`, or `none`.\n48\t## Reference Material\n49\t\n50\t### Reference 1\n51\t\n52\tRepository baseline at bundle generation time: the `autofdtd` repo has a minimal README, phase directories, and an effectively empty src tree. Phase 1 therefore starts from architecture, package skeleton, and subsystem contracts rather than refining an existing solver. The prep-generated feature ledger lives at `phase1/feature-checklist.md` and should be treated as input context for the loop, not as a loop deliverable.\n53\t\n54\t### Reference 2\n55\t\n56\tTidy3D EM feature surface identified from the local codebase and schemas: Simulation plus Scene and Structure containers; geometry families including Box, Sphere, Cylinder, PolySlab, GeometryGroup, Transformed, ClipOperation, GeometryArray, and TriangleMesh-related paths; material families including Medium, PECMedium, PMCMedium, custom and dispersive isotropic media, PoleResidue, Sellmeier, Lorentz, Drude, Debye, LossyMetalMedium, AnisotropicMedium, FullyAnisotropicMedium, CustomAnisotropicMedium, PerturbationMedium, and Medium2D; boundary families including Periodic, PECBoundary, PMCBoundary, BlochBoundary, PML, StablePML, Absorber, ABCBoundary, ModeABCBoundary, Boundary, and BoundarySpec; source families including GaussianPulse, ContinuousWave, CustomSourceTime, BroadbandPulse, UniformCurrentSource, PointDipole, CustomCurrentSource, CustomFieldSource, ModeSource, PlaneWave, GaussianBeam, AstigmaticGaussianBeam, and TFSF; monitor families including FieldMonitor, FieldTimeMonitor, AuxFieldTimeMonitor, MediumMonitor, PermittivityMonitor, FluxMonitor, FluxTimeMonitor, ModeMonitor, ModeSolverMonitor, GaussianOverlapMonitor, AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitor, FieldProjectionAngleMonitor, DirectivityMonitor, FieldProjectionCartesianMonitor, FieldProjectionKSpaceMonitor, DiffractionMonitor, SurfaceFieldMonitor, and SurfaceFieldTimeMonitor; and grid or subpixel controls including UniformGrid, CustomGridBoundaries, CustomGrid, AutoGrid, QuasiUniformGrid, GridRefinement, LayerRefinementSpec, GridSpec, ModeSpec, and SubpixelSpec with staircasing and averaging variants. High-value references: `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/simulation.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/structure.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/base.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/primitives.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/polyslab.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/current.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/field.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/time.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/monitor.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/medium.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/boundary.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/grid/grid_spec.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/mode_spec.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/subpixel_spec.py`, and `../tidy3d/schemas/Simulation.json`.\n57\t\n58\t### Reference 3\n59\t\n60\tTidy3D compatibility guidance for Phase 1: keep the public and IR models tagged, immutable, schema-driven, and validation-heavy; preserve named monitor access patterns and ordered structure semantics; and front-load an explicit feature matrix that distinguishes must-implement features, defer buckets, and reject-with-clear-error buckets rather than leaving gaps ambiguous.\n61\t\n62\t### Reference 4\n63\t\n64\tMeep architectural findings: the stable decomposition unit is the chunk rather than the rank; a rank may own multiple chunks; chunk ownership, symmetry reduction, and periodic or Bloch remapping behave like logical address transforms over a full-domain view; communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific; geometry and material initialization are separate from stepping; and near-to-far is a DFT-backed monitor family rather than a lightweight add-on. The Meep paper sharpens this further: the solver is designed to preserve an illusion of continuous space and time on top of chunked discrete execution, uses pervasive interpolation and restriction for sources, monitors, and field queries, relies on anisotropic subpixel smoothing to remove first-order interface error, and favors bulk region operations over pointwise APIs. High-value references: `../meep/doc/docs/Parallel_Meep.md`, `../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`, `../meep/src/structure.cpp`, `../meep/src/boundaries.cpp`, `../meep/src/fields.cpp`, `../meep/src/loop_in_chunks.cpp`, `../meep/src/near2far.cpp`, and `papers/meep_paper.pdf`.\n65\t\n66\t### Reference 5\n67\t\n68\tWarp backend findings: Warp kernels are Python-authored and JIT-compiled per module; all kernels in a module compile together and should be stabilized early to avoid reload churn. Arrays, vectors, matrices, and structs are strong fits for field buffers and metadata. CUDA launches are asynchronous; streams and events are first-class; graph capture and replay are realistic optimization targets for timestep loops; tile APIs are promising for later shared-memory optimizations but should not block a first Phase 1 backend; and built-in profiling via ScopedTimer, NVTX, and CUDA activity summaries is good enough for Phase 1 instrumentation. Benchmarking guidance from `papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf` adds that FDTD should be tracked primarily in cells updated per second or Gcells/s, usually as a bandwidth-bound workload, and that benchmark metadata must preserve grid shape, total cells, timestep count, precision, boundary or material class, single-versus-multi-GPU context, and separate end-to-end versus steady-state timing.\n69\t\n70\t### Reference 6\n71\t\n72\tScheduling findings from `papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`: a naive GPU FDTD is constrained mostly by memory traffic and synchronization, so the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy that may later delay or overlap neighbor consumption. Phase 1 should remain bulk-synchronous by default, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration should already preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics, interior-versus-boundary planning, and scheduler hooks so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n73\t\n74\t### Reference 7\n75\t\n76\tGeometry and mode-solver findings: GeometryPrimitives.jl is centered on analytic shape queries such as level, nearest surface point, normals, bounds, and translation rather than a full scene graph. Orthogonal prisms and convex polygons are natural early scope; subpixel support exists mainly through local plane-cut overlap ideas rather than a full arbitrary-shape averaging engine; KD-tree and periodic replication patterns matter early; and libctl provides richer object-model and overlap-integral precedents for material-carrying ordered geometry lists and periodic duplication. VectorModesolver.jl is best treated as a formulation and interface reference rather than a polished dependency surface. It expects a sampled tensor-valued permittivity callback over a rectilinear cross-section, assembles a sparse operator, solves for magnetic fields, and reconstructs electric fields.\n77\t\n78\t### Reference 8\n79\t\n80\tWorking-memory seed facts that later tasks should preserve: chunk is the architectural unit while rank or device is placement; stored topology and logical topology diverge because of symmetry and periodic or Bloch remapping; scene compilation must remain distinct from stepping; continuous problem specifications must lower through explicit interpolation and restriction into discrete execution artifacts; geometry lowering and materialization must precede serious kernels; subpixel smoothing can induce effective anisotropy even for physically isotropic materials; stage-specific halos and monitor metadata should be explicit; Tidy3D-style compatibility should be represented as a tagged immutable IR with normalization rules and unsupported-feature policy; runtime metrics should include Gcells/s plus separate end-to-end and steady-state timing; and near-to-far should be built on top of DFT monitor infrastructure rather than as an isolated feature.\n81\t\n82\t\n83\t## Runtime Context\n84\t\n85\t- The runner injects the current task's title, success criteria, and notes from `loop.spec.md` on every iteration. `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` now compile uniform current sources onto resolved primal-cell grids using overlap-or-interpolation axis placement, total-current normalization by transverse extent, and staged NumPy injection helpers for `(nx, ny, nz, 3)` electric or magnetic field buffers.\n170\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now lowers `UniformCurrentSource` into typed `UniformCurrentSourceIR` payloads instead of only the generic source envelope, and `src/autofdtd/examples/current_sources.py` plus `tests/test_sources_current.py` exercise placement, IR lowering, compilation, and runtime injection end to end.\n171\t- `src/autofdtd/sources/beam.py` now implements `GaussianBeam` and `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` with planar injection geometry, direction/polarization vectors, waist parameters, angular spec support (`FixedInPlaneKSpec`/`FixedAngleSpec`), and equivalence-principle injection.\n172\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` lowers `GaussianBeam` into `GaussianBeamIR` and `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` into `AstigmaticGaussianBeamIR` with beam-specific fields (waist radii, waist distances, dir/pol vectors).\n173\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/sources.py` provides `compile_gaussian_beam` and `compile_astigmatic_gaussian_beam` with `CompiledGaussianBeam`/`CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam` dataclasses including Gaussian envelope weights and k-vector functions.\n174\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/sources.py` provides `inject_gaussian_beam` and `inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam` using equivalence principle (J = n × H, M = -n × E) with spatially varying Gaussian envelope weights.\n175\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` provides `build_gaussian_beam_runtime`, `apply_gaussian_beam_sources`, `build_astigmatic_gaussian_beam_runtime`, `apply_astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources` for runtime integration.\n176\t- `tests/test_sources_beam.py` has 13 tests covering planar geometry validation, phase properties, direction vectors, waist validation, IR lowering, compilation, injection kernels, runtime integration, and angle support.\n177\t\n178\t## Monitor Facts (task-036)\n179\t\n180\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/models.py` now provides typed monitor models (`FieldMonitor`, `FluxMonitor`, `ModeMonitor`, etc.) with name normalization, unique-name validation, and interval/start semantics.\n181\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/__init__.py` exports the full monitor public API including `SimulationData`, `MonitorData`, `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData`, `PermittivityData`, and `monitor_model_from_value`.\n182\t- `SimulationData` provides Tidy3D-style named access: dictionary-style `sim_data[\"monitor_name\"]` and attribute-style `sim_data.monitor_name` for registered monitor data.\n183\t- `SimulationData` uses a `monitor_store` field (excluded from serialization) to hold runtime data, with registration methods (`register_field_monitor`, `register_flux_monitor`, etc.) that enforce unique-name constraints.\n184\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now has typed monitor IR models: `FieldMonitorIR`, `FieldTimeMonitorIR`, `AuxFieldTimeMonitorIR`, `FluxMonitorIR`, `FluxTimeMonitorIR`, `ModeMonitorIR`, `ModeSolverMonitorIR`, `MediumMonitorIR`, `PermittivityMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionAngleMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionCartesianMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionKSpaceMonitorIR`, `DiffractionMonitorIR`, `DirectivityMonitorIR`, `GaussianOverlapMonitorIR`, `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitorIR`, `SurfaceFieldMonitorIR`, `SurfaceFieldTimeMonitorIR`.\n185\t- `monitor_to_ir()` function in `ir/models.py` lowers concrete monitor models to typed IR payloads, falling back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown or incomplete monitor types.\n186\t- `SimulationIR.monitors` field is typed as `tuple[MonitorComponentIR, ...]` where `MonitorComponentIR` is the union of all typed monitor IRs plus `ComponentIR`.\n187\t- `src/autofdtd/core/validation.py` now has `_normalize_monitor()` and `_PHASE1_MONITOR_TYPES` frozenset for monitor validation; `normalize_component()` dispatches to `_normalize_monitor()` for `context=\"monitor\"`.\n188\t- `src/autofdtd/planning.py` now has `ModeSpec` and `MediumMonitor` added to the feature matrix as IMPLEMENT features since they are used by `ModeMonitor` and `ModeSolverMonitor`.\n189\t- Monitor data types (`FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData`, `PermittivityData`) store complex values as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n190\t\n191\t## Field Monitor Facts (task-037)\n192\t\n193\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFieldMonitor`, `FieldMonitorState`, `compile_field_monitor()` for field monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n194\t- `CompiledFieldMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, fields, interval, start) plus discrete placements and weights derived from the resolved grid.\n195\t- `FieldMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass that stores runtime recording state; time-domain monitors use `time_series` dict and `time_stamps` list, while frequency-domain monitors use `dft_data` dict and `dft_count` counter.\n196\t- Frozen dataclass field updates use `object.__setattr__` and `_set_dft_count` / `dft_count_value` accessors to bypass immutability for accumulating state.\n197\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `record_field_time_domain()`, `record_field_frequency_domain()`, `sample_field_at_points()`, and `accumulate_flux()` for field recording kernels.\n198\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_field_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_fields()`, and `extract_monitor_data()` for runtime field monitor orchestration.\n199\t- `monitor_to_ir()` is exported from `autofdtd.ir` for IR lowering of all monitor types.\n200\t- Field monitor compilation uses `_compile_monitor_axis_placement()` which derives placements from monitor center/size against grid boundaries.\n201\t- FieldMonitor with explicit `freqs` tuple is treated as frequency-domain (`is_frequency_domain=True`), otherwise it is not (even if monitor_type is FieldMonitor).\n202\t- Time-domain monitors record at every `interval` steps starting from `start` step; `record_monitor_fields()` handles the step filtering.\n203\t- `tests/test_monitors_field.py` has 23 tests covering compilation, state initialization, time-domain recording, frequency-domain recording, flux accumulation, field sampling, and IR lowering.\n204\t\n205\t## Flux Monitor Facts (task-039)\n206\t\n207\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n208\t- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n209\t- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n210\t- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n211\t- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n212\t- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n213\t- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n214\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n215\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n216\t- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n217\t- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n218\t\n219\t## Decisions\n220\t\n221\t- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n222\t- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n223\t- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n224\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n225\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n226\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n227\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n228\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n229\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n230\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n231\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n232\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n233\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n234\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n235\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n236\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n237\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n238\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n239\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n240\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n241\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n242\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n243\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n244\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n245\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n246\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n247\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n248\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n249\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n250\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n251\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n252\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n253\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n254\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n255\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n256\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n257\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n258\t\n259\t## Task Handoffs\n260\t\n261\t- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n262\t- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n263\t- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n264\t- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n265\t- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n266\t- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n267\t- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n268\t- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n269\t- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n270\t- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n271\t- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate tensor-material runtime for effective-media smoothing.\n272\t- Later absorbing-boundary tasks should extend `CompiledBoundarySpec` and the boundary ghost-update stage rather than reworking the public `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n273\t- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n274\t- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n275\t- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n276\t- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n277\t- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n278\t- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and lowering seam for nested `source_time` fields instead of reimplementing waveform parsing inside each source model.\n279\t- Later source-time expansions should preserve the current JSON-safe complex-pair representation for custom envelopes so package serialization and IR transport stay stable even if dataset-backed APIs are added later.\n280\t- Later `PointDipole` and `CustomCurrentSource` work should reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n281\t\n282\t## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n283\t\n284\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n285\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n286\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n287\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n288\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n289\t- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n290\t- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n291\t- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n292\t\n293\t## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n294\t\n295\t- Later monitor tasks (task-037 for field monitors, task-038 for medium monitors, task-039 for flux monitors, etc.) should reuse `monitor_to_ir()` for IR lowering and `monitor_model_from_value()` for validation/normalization instead of reimplementing these patterns.\n296\t- Later monitor tasks should extend `SimulationData` with specific registration methods (like `register_field_monitor`) and use `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n297\t- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n298\t- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is populated during simulation execution, not serialized with the simulation metadata.\n299\t- `Phase1_MONITOR_TYPES` in `core/validation.py` and `_MONITOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `monitors/models.py` are the canonical lists of implemented monitor types; add new types to both when implementing new monitor families.\n300\t\n301\t## Task Handoffs (continued)\n302\t\n303\t## Medium Monitor Facts (task-038)\n304\t\n305\t- `MediumMonitorData` model in `monitors/models.py` stores diagonal components (eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz, mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz) as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n306\t- `PermittivityMonitor` already had `PermittivityData` with real-valued eps components; `MediumMonitorData` extends with complex-valued permittivity + permeability.\n307\t- `SimulationData` now has `register_medium_monitor()` and `register_permittivity_monitor()` methods for data registration.\n308\t- `CompiledMediumMonitor` in `compiler/monitors.py` compiles medium/permittivity monitors onto resolved grids using the same axis-placement logic as field monitors.\n309\t- `MediumMonitorState` allocates a `(n_pts, num_freqs, n_components)` array at init: 6 components for MediumMonitor (eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz, mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz) or 3 for PermittivityMonitor.\n310\t- `MediumMonitorState.populate_from_scene()` can sample scene media at monitor placements and compute complex permittivity/permeability at each frequency.\n311\t\n312\t## GaussianOverlapMonitor Facts (task-041)\n313\t\n314\t- `GaussianOverlapMonitor` now exposes beam parameters (`angle_theta`, `angle_phi`, `pol_angle`, `waist_radius`, `waist_distance`) matching Tidy3D's `AbstractGaussianOverlapMonitor` interface.\n315\t- `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitor` now exposes beam parameters (`angle_theta`, `angle_phi`, `pol_angle`, `waist_sizes`, `waist_distances`) matching Tidy3D's `AbstractGaussianOverlapMonitor` interface.\n316\t- Both monitors use `_angles` property returning `(angle_theta, angle_phi)` tuple.\n317\t- `GaussianOverlapData` and `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapData` monitor data types store complex overlap amplitudes as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability, with first dimension being direction (+/-) and second being frequency.\n318\t- `GaussianOverlapMonitorIR` and `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitorIR` IR models now carry beam parameters and lower from monitor models via `monitor_to_ir()`.\n319\t- Both monitors are added to the feature matrix in `planning.py` as IMPLEMENT features.\n320\t- Phase 1 runtime/compiler support for overlap computation is deferred to future task-053 (monitor accumulation kernel stages) since the monitors have the full API surface, IR lowering, and data types in place.\n321\t- `SimulationData` now has `register_gaussian_overlap_monitor()` and `register_astigmatic_gaussian_overlap_monitor()` methods for data registration.\n322\t- `build_medium_monitor_runtime()` in `runtime/monitors.py` handles both MediumMonitor and PermittivityMonitor types, with appropriate defaulting for num_freqs/freqs.\n323\t- `extract_medium_monitor_data()` returns `MediumMonitorData` or `PermittivityData` depending on the monitor type.\n324\t- Phase 1 medium monitors sample static material properties (not dynamic field-dependent), so recording is a one-time scene sampling rather than per-timestep accumulation.\n325\t- IR lowering for MediumMonitor and PermittivityMonitor was already implemented in task-036 via `monitor_to_ir()`.\n326\t## Projection Monitor Facts (task-042)\n327\t\n328\t- `FieldProjectionAngleMonitor`, `FieldProjectionCartesianMonitor`, `FieldProjectionKSpaceMonitor`, `DiffractionMonitor`, and `DirectivityMonitor` now provide the full Tidy3D-style projection monitor public API surfaces.\n329\t- All five projection monitors are added to `planning.py` feature matrix as IMPLEMENT features.\n330\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/models.py` now provides data types `FieldProjectionAngleData`, `FieldProjectionCartesianData`, `FieldProjectionKSpaceData`, `DiffractionData`, and `DirectivityData` with JSON-safe `(real, imag)` tuple storage for complex values.\n331\t- `SimulationData` now has registration methods for all five projection monitor data types: `register_field_projection_angle_monitor`, `register_field_projection_cartesian_monitor`, Phase 1 therefore starts from architecture, package skeleton, and subsystem contracts rather than refining an existing solver. The prep-generated feature ledger lives at `phase1/feature-checklist.md` and should be treated as input context for the loop, not as a loop deliverable.\n\n### Reference 2\n\nTidy3D EM feature surface identified from the local codebase and schemas: Simulation plus Scene and Structure containers; geometry families including Box, Sphere, Cylinder, PolySlab, GeometryGroup, Transformed, ClipOperation, GeometryArray, and TriangleMesh-related paths; material families including Medium, PECMedium, PMCMedium, custom and dispersive isotropic media, PoleResidue, Sellmeier, Lorentz, Drude, Debye, LossyMetalMedium, AnisotropicMedium, FullyAnisotropicMedium, CustomAnisotropicMedium, PerturbationMedium, and Medium2D; boundary families including Periodic, PECBoundary, PMCBoundary, BlochBoundary, PML, StablePML, Absorber, ABCBoundary, ModeABCBoundary, Boundary, and BoundarySpec; source families including GaussianPulse, ContinuousWave, CustomSourceTime, BroadbandPulse, UniformCurrentSource, PointDipole, CustomCurrentSource, CustomFieldSource, ModeSource, PlaneWave, GaussianBeam, AstigmaticGaussianBeam, and TFSF; monitor families including FieldMonitor, FieldTimeMonitor, AuxFieldTimeMonitor, MediumMonitor, PermittivityMonitor, FluxMonitor, FluxTimeMonitor, ModeMonitor, ModeSolverMonitor, GaussianOverlapMonitor, AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitor, FieldProjectionAngleMonitor, DirectivityMonitor, FieldProjectionCartesianMonitor, FieldProjectionKSpaceMonitor, DiffractionMonitor, SurfaceFieldMonitor, and SurfaceFieldTimeMonitor; and grid or subpixel controls including UniformGrid, CustomGridBoundaries, CustomGrid, AutoGrid, QuasiUniformGrid, GridRefinement, LayerRefinementSpec, GridSpec, ModeSpec, and SubpixelSpec with staircasing and averaging variants. High-value references: `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/simulation.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/structure.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/base.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/primitives.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/geometry/polyslab.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/current.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/field.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/source/time.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/monitor.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/medium.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/boundary.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/grid/grid_spec.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/mode_spec.py`, `../tidy3d/tidy3d/components/subpixel_spec.py`, and `../tidy3d/schemas/Simulation.json`.\n\n### Reference 3\n\nTidy3D compatibility guidance for Phase 1: keep the public and IR models tagged, immutable, schema-driven, and validation-heavy; preserve named monitor access patterns and ordered structure semantics; and front-load an explicit feature matrix that distinguishes must-implement features, defer buckets, and reject-with-clear-error buckets rather than leaving gaps ambiguous.\n\n### Reference 4\n\nMeep architectural findings: the stable decomposition unit is the chunk rather than the rank; a rank may own multiple chunks; chunk ownership, symmetry reduction, and periodic or Bloch remapping behave like logical address transforms over a full-domain view; communication plans are stage-specific and component-family-specific; geometry and material initialization are separate from stepping; and near-to-far is a DFT-backed monitor family rather than a lightweight add-on. The Meep paper sharpens this further: the solver is designed to preserve an illusion of continuous space and time on top of chunked discrete execution, uses pervasive interpolation and restriction for sources, monitors, and field queries, relies on anisotropic subpixel smoothing to remove first-order interface error, and favors bulk region operations over pointwise APIs. High-value references: `../meep/doc/docs/Parallel_Meep.md`, `../meep/doc/docs/Chunks_and_Symmetry.md`, `../meep/src/structure.cpp`, `../meep/src/boundaries.cpp`, `../meep/src/fields.cpp`, `../meep/src/loop_in_chunks.cpp`, `../meep/src/near2far.cpp`, and `papers/meep_paper.pdf`.\n\n### Reference 5\n\nWarp backend findings: Warp kernels are Python-authored and JIT-compiled per module; all kernels in a module compile together and should be stabilized early to avoid reload churn. Arrays, vectors, matrices, and structs are strong fits for field buffers and metadata. CUDA launches are asynchronous; streams and events are first-class; graph capture and replay are realistic optimization targets for timestep loops; tile APIs are promising for later shared-memory optimizations but should not block a first Phase 1 backend; and built-in profiling via ScopedTimer, NVTX, and CUDA activity summaries is good enough for Phase 1 instrumentation. Benchmarking guidance from `papers/gpu_benchmarking.pdf` adds that FDTD should be tracked primarily in cells updated per second or Gcells/s, usually as a bandwidth-bound workload, and that benchmark metadata must preserve grid shape, total cells, timestep count, precision, boundary or material class, single-versus-multi-GPU context, and separate end-to-end versus steady-state timing.\n\n### Reference 6\n\nScheduling findings from `papers/systolic_fdtd.pdf`: a naive GPU FDTD is constrained mostly by memory traffic and synchronization, so the important architectural seam is the split between chunk-local interior work, lower-dimensional boundary exchange, and a pluggable execution policy that may later delay or overlap neighbor consumption. Phase 1 should remain bulk-synchronous by default, but chunk metadata and runtime orchestration should already preserve per-face, per-stage halo semantics, interior-versus-boundary planning, and scheduler hooks so future systolic or overlapped execution can be added without redesign.\n\n### Reference 7\n\nGeometry and mode-solver findings: GeometryPrimitives.jl is centered on analytic shape queries such as level, nearest surface point, normals, bounds, and translation rather than a full scene graph. Orthogonal prisms and convex polygons are natural early scope; subpixel support exists mainly through local plane-cut overlap ideas rather than a full arbitrary-shape averaging engine; KD-tree and periodic replication patterns matter early; and libctl provides richer object-model and overlap-integral precedents for material-carrying ordered geometry lists and periodic duplication. VectorModesolver.jl is best treated as a formulation and interface reference rather than a polished dependency surface. It expects a sampled tensor-valued permittivity callback over a rectilinear cross-section, assembles a sparse operator, solves for magnetic fields, and reconstructs electric fields.\n\n### Reference 8\n\nWorking-memory seed facts that later tasks should preserve: chunk is the architectural unit while rank or device is placement; stored topology and logical topology diverge because of symmetry and periodic or Bloch remapping; scene compilation must remain distinct from stepping; continuous problem specifications must lower through explicit interpolation and restriction into discrete execution artifacts; geometry lowering and materialization must precede serious kernels; subpixel smoothing can induce effective anisotropy even for physically isotropic materials; stage-specific halos and monitor metadata should be explicit; Tidy3D-style compatibility should be represented as a tagged immutable IR with normalization rules and unsupported-feature policy; runtime metrics should include Gcells/s plus separate end-to-end and steady-state timing; and near-to-far should be built on top of DFT monitor infrastructure rather than as an isolated feature.\n\n\n## Runtime Context\n\n- The runner injects the current task's title, success criteria, and notes from `loop.spec.md` on every iteration. Do not duplicate the full task catalog here.\n- Use `phase1/loop.spec.md` as the authoritative task board.\n- Use `phase1/feature-checklist.md` as the compact feature ledger for what Phase 1 should implement, defer, or reject clearly.\n- Use `phase1/kernel-formula-sources.md` as the compact source map for kernel formulas, update equations, and implementation references.\n- Keep the key architectural dependencies in mind while executing any task:\n  - mode solver work should precede `ModeSource` and mode-monitor workflows\n  - second-order subpixel smoothing depends on the anisotropic material representation and update path already working\n  - logging and convergence behavior are first-class runtime deliverables, not incidental byproducts\n  - kernel/runtime support must be explicit for materials, boundaries, sources, monitors, and postprocessing rather than implied by API support\n\n## Working Memory\n\n# Phase 1 Maxwell Solver Loop Working Memory\n\nUse this file for cumulative findings, reusable facts, decisions, and cross-task notes.\n\nKeep it concise. provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n\n## Task β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n\n## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n\n- Later monitor tasks SimulationData(TaggedModel):\n    \"\"\"Container for simulation results with Tidy3D-style named monitor access.\n\n    Provides dictionary-like access via ``sim_data['monitor_name']`` and\n    attribute access via ``sim_data.monitor_name`` for all monitor data\n    produced by a simulation run.\n\n    Example::\n\n        sim_data = SimulationData(simulation=sim, runtime_log=log)\n The grating equation for a 2D grating is:\n\nFor a surface with normal along axis `a`, the tangential axes are `b` and `c`. The grating has periods `d_b` and `d_c`.\n\nFor incident wave with k_inc = (0, 0, k0) (normal incidence):\n- k_diff_b = m_b * 2π / d_b\n- k_diff_c = m_c * 2π / d_c\n- k_diff_squared = ε * k0^2 - k_diff_b^2 - k_diff_c^2\n\nA propagating order requires k_diff_squared > 0.\n\n\nThe DiffractionMonitor doesn't actually store grating parameters directly—they come from the scene's geometry instead. So I need to either require the user to provide grating periods explicitly, or infer them from the scene during the computation. Given that task-042 already marked the full implementation as deferred, I'll defer the automatic inference and instead implement a practical approach that works for common cases. For Phase 1, I should support computing diffraction assuming the grating period matches the simulation grid spacing, which is a reasonable approximation for uniform structures.\n\nLooking at how the runtime extraction works in monitors.py, `extract_projection_monitor_data` already has access to the grid and can determine the surface's tangential cell sizes. The monitor sits at a surface where one dimension has zero size, so I can extract the period information directly from the grid along the tangential directions. I'll implement the diffraction order computation using this approach, then add the kernel function to handle the actual calculation.","signature":"fb1dbe59bbd0099af098c5a7a84320caa40cc5ed23028666d9b5b2974ecc9d89"}],"model":"MiniMax-M2.7","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":63294,"output_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":23367},"service_tier":"standard","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca414387-845a-4ea1-ab6f-068da29e2e1a","uuid":"42bb4c15-cc06-48ce-8855-25b9f0ffaeff"}

Phase 1 uses a simplified Rayleigh approximation:\n    # E_obs ~ (k0/r) * exp(-i*k0*r) * integral(Surface_J * exp(i*k0*r') dS')\n    # where Surface_J = n × H_surf for electric current\n    # and Surface_M = -n × E_surf for magnetic current\n\n    for i_phi, phi in enumerate(phi_vals):\n        for i_theta, theta in enumerate(theta_vals):\n            # Scalar products for this direction\n                    js_dot = js_x[i_freq] * obs_x + js_y[i_freq] * obs_y + js_z[i_freq] * obs_z\n                    ms_dot = ms_x[i_freq] * obs_x + ms_y[i_freq] * obs_y + ms_z[i_freq] * obs_z\n\n                    # Compute the spatial Fourier transform of the tangential field components\n3. Apply the grating equation to determine which orders are propagating\n4. Return the diffraction orders (amplitudes, mx, my)\n\nThe key physics:- For a 2D grating with periods dy and dz along the tangential directions- Diffraction order (mx, my) has k-vectors:  - k_y = mx * 2π/dy  - k_z = my * 2π/dz\n- k_squared = ε * k0^2 - k_y^2 - k_z^2\n- If k_squared > 0: propagating order- If k_squared < 0: evanescent order\n\nFor the amplitudes, the Fourier transform of the field at the monitor surface gives:- E_fourier(mx, my) = sum over surface of E_field * exp(-i*(k_y*y + k_z*z))\n\n\n- I'll integrate this with the runtime system by accepting the CompiledProjectionMonitor and grid, then computing the diffraction orders and returning DiffractionData with the computed amplitudes and order indices.","signature":"8e2dd1ad6ee2f321a8d75e8cbc58477c38546f0c4ccc53a1f81b110ea5d27d7d"}],"model":"MiniMax-M2.7","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":66267,"output_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":23367},"service_tier":"standard","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ca414387-845a-4ea1-ab6f-068da29e2e1a","uuid":"9317ef42-8259-4670-85b6-31a9abc8e201"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"0623e639a13828fb4f3c519a64090df9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"call_function_osmjlcybivdq_1","name":"Edit","input":{"replace_all":false,"file_path":"/workspace/autofdtd/src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py","old_string":"    return ex_out, ey_out, ez_out","new_string":"    return ex_out, ey_out, ez_out\n\n\ndef compute_diffraction_orders(\n    dft_e: np.ndarray,\n    dft_h: np.ndarray,\n    placements: tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...],\n    normal_axis: int,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n    *,\n    period_y: float | None = None,\n    period_z: float | None = None,\n    medium_eps: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j,\n    num_orders: int = 5,\n) -> tuple[\n    np.ndarray,  # orders: (num_orders, num_freqs) complex\n    tuple[int, ...],  # mx indices\n    tuple[int, ...],  # my indices\n]:\n    \"\"\"Compute diffraction orders from surface DFT fields.\n\n    This function applies the grating equation to determine diffraction\n    order amplitudes from fields recorded on a 2D surface. The surface\n    acts as a 2D Fourier transform element, projecting the field\n    distribution into angular spectrum components.\n\n    Physics\n    -------\n    For a 2D surface with periodicity along y and z directions:\n    - k_y = m_y * 2π / d_y\n    - k_z = m_z * 2π / d_z\n    - k_diff^2 = ε * k0^2 - k_y^2 - k_z^2\n\n    A propagating order requires k_diff^2 > 0 (real k_diff).\n    Evanescent orders (k_diff^2 < 0) are suppressed.\n\n    Args:\n        dft_e: DFT of E fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        dft_h: DFT of H fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        placements: Surface cell indices (x, y, z)\n        normal_axis: Surface normal axis (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        freqs: Frequency values for each DFT component\n        period_y: Grating period along y direction (None = use grid spacing)\n        period_z: Grating period along z direction (None = use grid spacing)\n        medium_eps: Relative permittivity of the diffraction medium\n        num_orders: Number of diffraction orders to compute (each side of zero)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (orders, mx, my) where:\n        - orders: complex array (num_orders^2, num_freqs) with diffraction amplitudes\n        - mx: integer tuple of m_x indices for each order\n        - my: integer tuple of m_y indices for each order\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    n_freqs = len(freqs)\n    c0 = 299792458.0  # speed of light in m/s\n\n    # Get tangential axes (the non-normal directions)\n    if normal_axis == 0:\n        tang1, tang2 = 1, 2  # y, z\n        e_tang1 = 1  # Ey\n        e_tang2 = 2  # Ez\n        h_tang1 = 1  # Hy\n        h_tang2 = 2  # Hz\n    elif normal_axis == 1:\n        tang1, tang2 = 0, 2  # x, z\n        e_tang1 = 0  # Ex\n        e_tang2 = 2  # Ez\n        h_tang1 = 0  # Hx\n        h_tang2 = 2  # Hz\n    else:  # normal_axis == 2\n        tang1, tang2 = 0, 1  # x, y\n        e_tang1 = 0  # Ex\n        e_tang2 = 1  # Ey\n        h_tang1 = 0  # Hx\n        h_tang2 = 1  # Hy\n\n    # If periods not provided, we can't compute proper diffraction orders\n    # Return zero amplitudes with a warning\n    if period_y is None or period_z is None or period_y <= 0 or period_z <= 0:\n        # No valid periodicity - return empty orders\n        n_orders = num_orders * 2 + 1\n        mx_vals = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n        my_vals = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n        orders = np.zeros((n_orders, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n        return orders, mx_vals, my_vals\n\n    # Compute k0 for each frequency\n    k0_vals = 2.0 * np.pi * np.array(freqs) / c0\n\n    # Generate diffraction order indices\n    mx_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n    my_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n    n_orders = len(mx_all) * len(my_all)\n\n    # Prepare output arrays\n    orders = np.zeros((n_orders, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    mx_out = []\n    my_out = []\n\n    # Compute spatial frequencies for each diffraction order\n    # k_y = m_y * 2π / period_y\n    # k_z = m_z * 2π / period_z\n    order_idx = 0\n    for mx in mx_all:\n        for my in my_all:\n            k_y = mx * 2.0 * np.pi / period_y\n            k_z = my * 2.0 * np.pi / period_z\n            k_yz_sq = k_y**2 + k_z**2\n\n            mx_out.append(mx)\n            my_out.append(my)\n\n            # For each frequency, compute amplitude for this order\n            for i_freq in range(n_freqs):\n                k0 = k0_vals[i_freq]\n\n                # Check if propagating: k_yz_sq < |ε| * k0^2\n                # For complex ε, use real part for propagation criterion\n                eps_real = medium_eps.real\n                if k_yz_sq > eps_real * k0**2:\n                    # Evanescent order - Total flux value\n    \"\"\"\n    sign = 1.0 if direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n\n    # For a surface perpendicular to `axis`, flux = integral of\n    # the normal component of the Poynting vector S = E × H\n    # The normal component depends on the axis:\n    # - x-axis: S_x = E_y * H_z - E_z * H_y\n    # - y-axis: S_y = E_z * H_x - E_x * H_z\n    # - z-axis: S_z = E_x * H_y - E_y * H_x\n\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != axis)\n\n    flux = 0.0\n    for idx in placements:\n        ex = electric_field[idx][0]\n        ey = electric_field[idx][1]\n        ez = electric_field[idx][2]\n        hx = magnetic_field[idx][0]\n        hy = magnetic_field[idx][1]\n        hz = magnetic_field[idx][2]\n\n        if axis == 0:\n            s_normal = ey * hz - ez * hy\n        elif axis == 1:\n            s_normal = ez * hx - ex * hz\n        else:  # axis == 2\n            s_normal = ex * hy - ey * hx\n\n        flux += s_normal.real\n\n    return sign * flux\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Flux monitor kernels\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef flux_monitor_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, object]:\n    \"\"\"Expose the flux monitor kernel backend choices for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"backend\": \"numpy\",\n        \"warp_available\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"supports_graph_capture\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"staging\": (\"flux_integration\",),\n    }\n\n\ndef record_flux_time_domain(\n    electric_field: electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        monitor_state: FluxMonitorState with compiled placement and direction\n        time: Current simulation time\n    \"\"\"\n    if not hasattr(monitor_state, \"compiled\"):\n        return\n\n    compiled = monitor_state.compiled\n\n    if not compiled.is_time_domain:\n        return\n\n    flux_value = accumulate_flux(\n        electric_field,\n        magnetic_field,\n        compiled.direction,\n        compiled.normal_axis,\n        compiled.placements,\n    )\n\n    if monitor_state.flux_series is not None:\n        monitor_state.flux_series.append(flux_value)\n    if monitor_state.time_stamps is not None:\n        monitor_state.time_stamps.append(time)\n\n\ndef record_flux_frequency_domain(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    monitor_state,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n) -> field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        monitor_state: FluxMonitorState with compiled placement and direction\n        time: Current simulation time\n    \"\"\"\n    if not hasattr(monitor_state, \"compiled\"):\n        return\n\n    compiled = monitor_state.compiled\n\n    if not compiled.is_frequency_domain:\n        return\n\n    flux_value = accumulate_flux(\n        electric_field,\n        magnetic_field,\n        compiled.direction,\n        compiled.normal_axis,\n        compiled.placements,\n    )\n\n    if monitor_state.dft_flux is not None:\n        omega_t None:\n    \"\"\"Record medium properties for a monitor.\n\n    For medium monitors, the medium properties are static (computed once\n    at setup from the scene), so this function just checks whether the\n    step index matches the interval/start criteria.\n\n    Args:\n        monitor_state: MediumMonitorState with compiled medium data\n        step_index: Current step index\n\n    Returns:\n        True if the monitor should record at this step, False otherwise\n    \"\"\"\n    compiled = monitor_state.compiled\n\n    # Check if this step should be recorded\n    if step_index <
